This is a bit of a transition chapter.

Enjoy?

 

 

 

 

She hates it when Lena’s like this—it absolutely grates on her nerves.

The worst part is she can’t bring herself to be mad at Lena, because she understands.

Lena is, at least to Kara, the kindest, warmest, most loving person she knows. It’s why, despite all her issues, despite always feeling like her world is going to fall apart any second, Kara knows with absolute certainty that Lena is the one person she can turn to.

But Lena is also proud, stubborn, and can suffer from a very special case of tunnel vision. Special, in this case, meaning that Lena can tunnel into one specific topic while keeping Kara on the periphery.

Which is exactly what she’s doing right now, judging by the look on her face and the unnatural lilt of her voice. She knows Lena is fuming mad. By the look of that vein in her forehead and the chokehold she has on her phone, Kara suspects that Lena is just a hair trigger away from going on a murder spree…and getting away with it.

And when she speaks, it sounds as if almost all forms of kindness are being painfully squeezed out of her so that when the words come out, they’re still warm but choked.

“Go ahead, Kara. I’ll be with you,” she says, gesturing toward the small elevator that will take them inside, her voice sounding simultaneously warm and cold.

Kara quickly glances at Taja, who is focused on Lena, before she returns to Lena. “You said ‘Rhea’’” she prompts.

Lena looks hesitant for a second, despite the stony exterior. She’s never seen Lena look this… cold, devoid of any other emotion. Taja, standing next to Lena, wears an equally arctic expression. If she didn’t know the two of them, she’d be terrified.

But she does know them, and these stony versions fill Kara with dread—not for herself, not really. She knows she’s safe with them, but that also means they’re trying to shut her out, to keep her safe from whatever it is that’s forced them to reveal this side of themselves.

Lena heads toward the elevator with Taja a step behind her. She looks at Kara expectantly before Kara follows suit.

“Rhea has released a statement, sent to L-Corp’s board, employees, partners, investors, and all major news sites, alleging that I knew all about Lex’s activities, that I’m just as greedy, possibly worse because I testified against him,” Lena says in a carefully controlled tone as soon as the elevator doors closed. “This argument has already been dealt with in court, of course, but that’s not going to stop her. I haven’t seen the new evidence she claims to have, but I’m also certain most of it is falsified or taken completely out of context.”

Kara’s heart falters for a bit before something grips it tight.

“Do you think Mike is behind this?” she asks, not missing Taja’s glance at her.

“He knows about the two of you?” Taja asks, her gaze alternating between Kara and Lena. Kara can’t even pretend to be surprised that Taja knows about her and Lena.

The elevator door opens and it is Taja who walks out first.

“We recently acknowledged it,” Lena answers evenly, stepping out after Taja. She turns to Kara. “It’s definitely a possibility.”

They walk in silence along suspiciously empty corridors, and the few people who do cross their path suddenly find themselves turning into the next hallway or busy with their phones or tablets, anything that gives them an excuse not to look at the intimidating trio.

By the time they reach the lobby of Dr. Hopper’s office, Scarlet is there, looking ominous and imposing beside the poor receptionist who nods and smiles a thin, watery smile at Taja, not even acknowledging Lena or Kara.

Kara is almost certain Taja is behind the privacy and the empty corridors.

“I’ll be waiting here,” Lena says, waving at the couch near the receptionist’s station.

Kara takes one look at Lena, then Taja and Scarlet. All three of them wearing what they think must be reassuring smiles. They look more like villains plotting murder while pressing a reassuring kiss to their favorite child as she hops on the bus for school.

 

 

 

 

Despite everything that just happened, Dr. Hopper is okay, Kara decides, after only ten minutes.

He’s balding, soft-spoken, with curly, brown hair, and looks entirely forgettable that as soon as Kara steps out of his office, closing the door behind her, she’ can’t quite summon a vision of him in her head.

His office is just as unmemorable as he is, but his couch is comfortable. Hardwood floors, shelves with leatherbound books, a simple wooden desk in the far left corner with a closed laptop and just one picture frame. Kara thinks she remembers multiple diplomas on the wall.

But at least he’s better than all the therapists she’s had before. He doesn’t push too much or try to lead Kara, content in just letting her talk, only interrupting when she goes off on a tangent.

For the most part, she just talks about her life and why she’s seeking counseling.

“I’ve been in and out of therapy for years. I don’t know… I just couldn’t commit to it,” she says.

“What’s changed? Why are you suddenly willing to commit now?” His voice is calm and inviting.

“I don’t want my child to suffer because I didn’t ask for help,” she pauses to take a breath. “And as much as my partner is understanding and patient, I don’t want to burden my child and my partner about this more than necessary. I know it’s going to be long and arduous, but I think I’ve found two good reasons to stay this time.”

Dr. Hopper nods.

“You’re not going to tell me I should want this for myself?” Kara asks, askance.

“Do you?”

“Of course, I do.”

Dr. Hopper smiles his warm smile. “There you go, then. Wanting something for yourself is good and all, but sometimes, we find a reason that’s far bigger than simply wanting it, and that’s just as good, don’t you think?”

“Aren’t you afraid that I’ll quit our sessions if my partner and I break up?”

Dr. Hopper shrugs. “That happens. But I’ll take whatever reasons people have for wanting to go to therapy as good and valid. We’ll work on everything else in time. What’s important is you’re here now, seeking help.”

No, she doesn’t remember much of what Dr. Hopper looks like, beyond his balding head, but as she steps out of his office and sees Lena look up to her, she feels the echo of their conversation.

 

 

 

 

Taja and Scarlet aren’t there when her session ends, and Lena had been looking at her phone with the same murderous expression she’s had since she turned the damned thing on about an hour earlier.

It is quickly replaced by something decidedly warmer, however, when Lena looks up and finds Kara staring at her.

“I know you wanted to go on a date, but maybe we can just have something delivered and eat at home?” Kara suggests as she walks to her. Lena stands and holds out her arm for Kara.

“Are you sure?” Lena asks, pocketing her phone.

“I’m sure,” Kara nods.

Lena, understanding, kisses Kara’s temple, murmuring a soft “Thank you,” before leading her back to the parking lot where Scarlet is dutifully waiting, standing like a sentinel beside an idling car.

“Home, please, Scarlet,” Kara says, kindly. Scarlet nods once at her and opens the door with a slight bow.

The drive home is quiet, with Kara ordering on the way, one hand holding Lena’s.

Over dinner, unable to stop herself, Kara finally asks.

“How bad is it?”

For a second, it looks like Lena is going to dismiss the question, but she takes a deep breath, lowers her fork, and wipes her mouth while her expression hardens.

“As I predicted, most of her alleged new evidence lacks context, is circumstantial at best, or has been proven false in court. But my board has been out for my blood ever since I took over, so I’m pretty sure they’re going to latch onto everything she puts forth as ‘New Evidence,’” Lena says, with the same clinical tone that Taja uses during their clinic visits. “But it’s not just that.” And here she hesitates, taking a couple of seconds before continuing. “She’s also obtained some rather sensitive photos of me with other people. They were from the days long before taking over L-Corp and a few of them are… indelicate.”

The careful phrasing tells Kara pretty much everything she needs to know.

She swallows, trying hard not to imagine what the pictures might look like, but she has a sinking feeling she knows exactly where they came from. “Mike’s handiwork?”

“Almost certainly.”

The flat confirmation sinks her heart to the bottom of her stomach. Not that it should come as a surprise, but for Lena to come to the same conclusion with near certainty just makes it that much more painful… but she should have seen this coming, right? It’s the same fucking cycle all over again. She’s once again unwittingly put the lives of those she loves in peril… The only smart thing to do would be to distance herself.

Because it has to be her fault in the end, after all. Lena would never have been in this situation if they hadn’t met. Rhea would’ve never dared touch her because Kara would’ve never met Mike. Everyone would be going on with their lives, blissfully untouched by the devastation that somehow always follows Kara.

“I’m sorry, Lena…” She exhales.

And she knows this is unhealthy, previous therapy sessions had taught her this much, at least. But they never taught her how to reverse the spiral. She knows, logically, that it can’t be her fault. She is not in control of what Rhea and Mike are doing. But she can’t help this sinking feeling that everyone would have been better off—

“I need you here, Kara.”

It’s the quiet assurance in Lena’s voice that breaks her out it, an assurance that Kara is wanted, needed.

“I know you think this is your fault, but it’s really not. I need you to know that. You have to know that,” Lena continues, reaching from the other side of the table and cupping Kara’s jaw, her thumb gently rubbing Kara’s cheek. “And besides, I’d rather have this messy, chaotic life with you and the Little Hatchling than have everything go smoothly and not have you two.”

Kara hesitates. “Just… promise me two things, Lena?”

Lena looks at her and nods.

“Promise me you won’t fight this alone. You’ll let us help you…or you’ll at least let me help you. Don’t keep anything from me even if you think it might hurt me.” She pauses and looks at Lena who is staring at her intently. “Also, promise me that when we’re home, you’ll allow yourself to be vulnerable and tired,” Kara says. She takes another deep breath. “And I promise you I won’t wallow in self-pity or blame myself. I won’t run. But when I feel like running, I’ll tell you.”

Lena looks at Kara like she’s a lungful of fresh air after drowning for so long.

That night Lena fucks her as if Kara’s life depended on it, leaving Kara a boneless mess, sated and sighing.

Because even in the face of a brewing storm, Lena still sees Kara’s needs as paramount to whatever else is happening. And for that, Kara will always be grateful.

 

 

 

In the days that follow Lena struggles to keep her promise… and yet, for all her pride and stubbornness, Lena never keeps her out of the loop, and Kara is proud of her for that.

As Kara anticipated, it isn’t so much the problem with Lena’s board that upsets her girlfriend than the fact that Lena’s life is once again in the limelight negatively. She’s used to fighting the board at every turn, even though she’s done nothing but prove herself right over and over. But now, her reputation is again thrust upon the court of public opinion, one she’s worked so hard to scrub clean.

“It’s not even that people think less of me,” Lena says, one evening as they sit on the couch.

Kara is having a late snack as she watches Lena emerge from her study, looking harassed and sleepless, with bags under her red, tired eyes. Instead of talking about what’s bothering her, Lena elects to just watch whatever is on Netflix.

It takes Kara a little over thirty minutes to convince Lena to just let it all out, with Lena arguing that all the negativity around her can’t be good for the baby. Kara wins with a pout and a crinkle in her forehead.

“I can take negative publicity. I’m used to it. Lex made sure of that.” She grits her teeth, her jaws tightening, before leaning to lay her head on Kara’s lap. Slightly grateful for her reflexes, Kara immediately removes the fruit plate on her lap to make room for Lena. She strokes Lena’s hair and is quite pleased to see Lena relax slightly.

“He ruined our name, broke Mother’s and my heart, and destroyed the company our parents worked so hard to build,” Lena continues, tears brimming. She closes her eyes and Kara watches as Lena desperately fights her tears back. “It took years to get the employees, the investors, and the general public to trust in the Luthor name again. So, no… I don’t care what they say about me if it only affected me… I’m rich enough for the three of us to never worry about money for the rest of our lives. But I worry for the employees and the people who have decided to trust us with their money.”

The sigh she produces simultaneously breaks Kara’s heart and fills her with so much anger that not even rougher-than-usual sex that leaves them both marked, bruised, and deliciously hurting in a lot of places calms Kara down.

If anything, she wakes up the next day, watches as Lena takes another early morning phone call already highly stressed, and this only compounds her anger.

 

 

 

The worst part is that with the number of reporters and paparazzi hounding Lena, Kara is being deprived of one of her favorite parts of their day, the part where Lena drives her to work and walks her to her desk.

And because Lena is overprotective of Kara, and, as has previously been established, Taja takes care of everything and everyone that Lena treasures, the role of guarding Kara and driving her to and from work suddenly falls on Scarlet and Taja’s Phantom.

This, of course, is decided after Lena is unable to pick Kara up from work and had to ask Scarlet to pick her up, despite assurances from Kara that she can take a cab home. Taja is on the phone that evening, barely listening to any of Kara’s protests.

“I can’t have Scarlet guarding me all the time, Taja. I have a job and I can’t have her loitering around the office or even just waiting for me in the parking lot all the time. Besides, I’m not sure how I’m going to explain to my boss how I’m suddenly able to afford a full-time bodyguard and driver—”

“I’ll deal with Cat Grant.” Taja’s voice on the other line is clipped and stern.

“But shouldn’t Lena have the better protection here? She’s the one being chased and harassed by reporters. Besides, between the two of us, you’re the more valuable one and I’m—”

“Carrying my future niece, possibly even the heir to all my fortunes when I most likely forget to have children because I’m too busy not having them,” Taja answers, cutting her off for the second time. Before Kara is able to respond, Taja continues, “But if it’s me and Lena you’re more concerned with, I’m more than happy to hire an entire army to guard the two of us, just to convince you to let Scarlet do her job.”

And true to her word, the next morning, instead of Lena driving her to work, Kara is shocked to find Taja waiting for her with Scarlet.

“Taja?”

“Get in. You’re going to be late,” Taja says, barely sparing a glance at Kara as her fingers fly on the laptop she’s working on. The drive from their apartment to CatCo is quiet but not uncomfortable. Taja’s focus is on her laptop and on her phone.

To Kara’s horror, upon arriving at CatCo, Taja does the exact same thing Lena usually does and grabs her bags. She walks Kara to her desk, oblivious to Kara’s protests and the stares and whispers of everyone around them, drops her bags and waits until Kara is seated before she turns towards Cat Grant’s office, enters, sits on one of the couches, and waits there, as if she owns the place.

It suddenly occurs to Kara that Taja might actually own a huge part of CatCo.

Meanwhile, Scarlet takes the same chair as before, and puts it in the same place she did the last time, takes one long scan of her environment before pulling her Kindle out from an inside jacket pocket and settling down as if she’s sat there all her life.

Kara knows better than to ask so instead, she calls her boss who, above everything, hates surprises. Cat, upon hearing who is in her office, immediately tells Kara to make sure that everything Taja asks for is given to her, no questions asked and to let Taja know that she’ll be there in less than fifteen minutes. She hears Cat muttering, wondering what Taja could possibly want before the call disconnects.

Kara stands and opens Cat’s office, half her body leaning inside.

“Taja, would you like anything? Tea? Coffee?” she asks. But Taja merely smoothes her blouse, bows her head a little and says, “I’m fine, but if Cat Grant asks, I’ll tell her you offered.”

“She also says she’ll be here in fifteen minutes,” Kara adds, lingering by the doorway.

Taja looks at her, then at her boss’ desk. “Have you told her about the Little Hatchling?”

Kara fidgets, before fully entering Cat Grant’s office, gently closing the glass door behind her.

“I’ll take that as a ‘No,’” Taja says, as soon as the doors close. “When do you plan to tell her?”

“I’m not sure,” she answers. “I should do it soon.”

“That would be smart,” Taja nods, her signature smirk back now. “If you like, I can pull some strings to get you maternity leave for the duration of your pregnancy. I’m sure I can convince Cat Grant to hire an intern while you rest and focus on you and the Little Hatchling. You can convince Lena Luthor to go with you, maybe have a vacation. I’m sure she has an island or two you could escape to…” The suggestion isn’t lost on Kara and she blushes, which Taja immediately picks up on. “I see you know what I’m talking about.”

Kara takes another step forward. “I’d really like that, to be honest, what with all the mess that Rhea’s stirring up. Lena can definitely use the break. But I also know that Lena loves her company and her employees and would never leave when it’s in trouble. Besides, Cat Grant has been more than accommodating and kind to me, and I can’t abuse our relationship like that.”

Taja nods and there’s suddenly a moment of silence that lasts long enough that Kara decides it’s her cue to leave Taja to her thoughts.

“If you need anything else,” Kara tilts her head towards her station outside Cat Grant’s office, “You know where to find me.”

She makes to turn around when Taja speaks up, the expression on her face almost hesitant.

“Now that we have a few minutes to ourselves, I’d like to ask something that you are free not to answer if you feel it’s any way invasive or improper,” Taja starts, her hand gesturing to the couch directly in front of where she’s sitting.

Kara looks outside, noting a few intrigued glances at the two of them from the people who have a direct line of sight to Cat Grant’s office. She already dreads the incoming barrage of questions later at lunch but there’s really nothing she can do about it now.

She walks and sits where Taja is gesturing and takes a steadying breath. “Okay…” she says, carefully. She suspects she’s about to have whatever is the rich people version of a shovel talk coming from someone who genuinely loves Lena… and who also appears to have a rather wide sphere of influence.

“What’s your plan after having the Little Hatchling? And I’m only asking because I’m pretty sure Lena Luthor’s going to ask you to marry her. There’s no doubt in my mind about that. However, I’m sure you’re aware that being married to someone as high profile as Lena Luthor has consequences.”

Right then, Kara realizes the one thing that annoys her about Taja, beyond her quietly forceful nature and the fact that she cannot take ‘No’ for an answer when she’s absolutely certain she’s correct.

Taja, at least in Kara’s eyes, despite being somewhat socially inept, is unnaturally good at reading people. And she uses this to her advantage, predicting people’s movements and reactions long before they themselves even consider their next move.

…which means Kara cannot lie. She cannot bullshit her way through an acceptable answer because she knows Taja will see through it.

“Before I met Lena and Mike, I already had a plan,” Kara starts. “I thought about working for a few years at CatCo, learn as much as I can about the media and writing, while saving enough to go back to college.” She takes a centering breath and Taja’s unwavering expression gives her encouragement.

“I had a decent amount in savings before Mike happened, and then I had to dip into it just to support him while he was looking for a job. I thought that when he finally landed the job at CatCo, I’d be able to save up again. But we moved in together and had to find a slightly bigger apartment, and I still ended up paying majority of the rent most of the time. Almost a year before I found out I was pregnant, my savings were already gone.”

Kara had watched with increasing despair as her college fund was slowly whittled down to nothing. But at the time, she was still convinced that Mike was the next best thing. She had still somehow loved him enough to believe that he’d change for the better, that he’d grow up and start pulling his own weight. Even the fact that Mike hit her and forced himself on her were not enough to make her break away.

But the thought that Mike really was selfish enough to not want to support his own child finally forced Kara’s hand. Sure, she can take him being selfish when it’s just her life on the line. But there are two of them now for him to abandon…

Kara shakes her head, feeling the anger rising again.

“So, to answer your question, I plan on finishing my degree,” she says with resolve. “I don’t plan on being a secretary all my life, not that there’s anything wrong with that. It’s just not what I dreamt of doing. The reason I wanted to work at CatCo to begin with was because I wanted to be a journalist. NCU has an excellent journalism program. Initially, I planned to ask Ms. Grant if I could work part-time here while I go back to college, but with the Little Hatchling I think I have to give up my job so I can juggle my baby and school and hope that Ms. Grant takes me back or at least gives me a good enough recommendation and reference when I finally start my career,” Kara finishes with a slow exhale.

Taja takes a few seconds, probably to gather her thoughts, Kara suspects.

“An excellent plan,” Taja finally says, folding her hands on top of her lap.  “And with you living with Lena Luthor, I’m guessing you’ve hardly touched your salary, which means you could at least get back a portion of your savings and invest it in your education. We’ll cover the rest.”

Which is exactly what Kara was afraid she would say.

Kara, for all her anxieties and traumas, is more than aware that she’s an outsider to the world and social circles that Lena and Taja navigate with ease. And while she’s allowed Lena to pretty much pay for everything (mostly because she’s tired of trying to actually pay for anything, except that one time she won on a technicality), she doesn’t want anyone to think that she’s only with Lena for her money.

“Actually, I’m planning on getting a loan for the rest of it,” Kara says. And again, with Kara being unable to stop Lena from paying for everything else, Kara is sure she’ll be able to pay off her loans in about a year, provided she actually gets a decent job.

“I thought you’d say that,” Taja answers, but if she really is displeased with Kara’s answer, it isn’t obvious. Instead, she stands up and holds out her arm for Kara to take. Kara hesitates for a second before she takes it and Taja walks her to the door.

“I will not have time for pleasantries after my meeting with Cat Grant. My other car is already idling outside. Please do not think I’m rude if I walk past you without saying goodbye. Also, Alex Danvers and Kelly Olsen will be flying out this Friday evening and will be staying at my home for the rest of the weekend. I haven’t informed Lena Luthor yet, but I’m hoping we can have dinner on Saturday, after your appointment with me.”

Kara narrows her eyes.

“You’re telling me this to convince Lena, aren’t you?”

Taja’s smile is a touch brighter. “Lena Luthor has chosen her wife wisely.”

Kara shakes her head. “I’ll see what I can do, but you know she’s been so busy that she’s had to rearrange her schedule so many times just to make time for our appointment.”

Taja nods, her smile suddenly grim. “I’m well aware. But that’s why I trust you can somehow convince her. If not, then at least you should still come join us. It would be nice to have you over for dinner what with your sister being there and all.”

Kara nods, hesitant. She’s still not sure how Taja managed to convince Alex and Kelly to stay with her, a perfect stranger, over the weekend, but Kara can’t deny that she’s at least intrigued to find out where this eccentric little heiress and her tall, lanky bodyguard live.

“I’ll do my best and let you know as soon as possible,” Kara says before ducking out of her boss’ office and back to her desk where Scarlet doesn’t even spare her a glance, seemingly absorbed in whatever it is that she’s reading.

A few minutes later Cat Grant walks out of her private elevator, her eyes darting from Taja, waiting inside the glass-walled office, to Kara, who is already jogging towards her, and Scarlet, oblivious to everything.

“How long has she been waiting?” Cat asks, muttering lowly as she hands over a few documents to Kara while they stroll toward her office.

“About thirteen minutes,” Kara replies with the same volume. “I did say you’d be here in fifteen.”

Cat nods, “Good. She didn’t ask for anything?”

“She declined refreshments.”

“Come to my office after five minutes and remind me about my meeting with RSM.”

There’s no meeting with RSM. There’s no RSM at all. But it’s her boss’ way of ditching a meeting early without seeming rude. Kara, however, suspects that this meeting won’t even last long enough for Kara to come in with the RSM excuse. She nods anyway as they split ways before reaching Cat Grant’s office door.

“I bet you two minutes,” Scarlet says, not looking up.

“Two and a half. Whoever is closer wins. Loser buys lunch,” Kara answers, smirking.

“Unacceptable. I’ll buy lunch regardless,” Scarlet responds, her eyes still on her book.

“Okay, then if I win, you can’t call me master anymore,” Kara says, staring at Scarlet, anticipating a push back.

“Acceptable, but only in private. When my Master is around, I still have to call you Master Danvers. But if I win,” and here Scarlet pauses, a tiny smile forming on her lips, her eyes still tracing the lines on her book. “If I win, you will let me carry your bags and you will hold on to my arm without any resistance whenever I am assigned to protect you and drive you around.”

Kara narrows her eyes, suddenly feeling like she’s been scammed.

 

 

 

Kara is quite sure she’s been scammed when Taja walks out of her boss’ office exactly two minutes later. As Taja had said earlier, she doesn’t even acknowledge Kara’s nor Scarlet’s presence as she’s already on her phone, whispering rather urgently to whoever is on the other line. She barely has time to ponder things, however, as she hears her phone ring, Cat Grant’s extension flashing on its screen.

She hurries inside, her notebook and pen in hand.

“Interesting talk I just had,” Cat Grant says, sitting behind her vast wooden desk, tapping her fingers, an unreadable expression on her face. Kara doesn’t respond.

“I’m assuming you know exactly who that woman is, seeing as her bodyguard is sitting behind you,” Cat raises an eyebrow and Kara nods.

“Good…” Cat drawls and pauses before continuing. “How long have you two been dating?”

Kara nearly does a spit take on just air. Still, she sputters, her brain freezing for a second before she fully takes in her boss’ question.

“She—She’s not my girlfriend!” Kara manages to stammer out.

“Oh?”

“She’s not,” she insists. “She’s Lena’s childhood friend and with all that’s happening around Lena, she’s volunteered her—uhmm—Scarlet…” she gestures at Scarlet’s sitting figure, “—to drive me to and from work…and to also be my bodyguard.”

 “Hmm…” Cat responds, clearly unconvinced. “Do you have any idea the people you are running with? Reporters would kill for that kind of connection, and yet here you are…”

“I wouldn’t have met them without you, Ms. Grant,” Kara answers, earnestly. “I met Lena when you sent me to have her look over and sign a few things, and we somehow became friends—”

“And now, after dancing around your feelings for each other for so long, you finally got rid of that man child and are now dating the Lena Luthor. Don’t even bother to deny it, Ms. Danvers,” her boss says, waving a hand.

Kara wasn’t about to deny it, to be honest. But she’s learned that there are times when it’s better to let her boss thinks she’s right. This is probably one of those times.

“Does everyone in the office know?” Kara asks.

Cat scoffs. “As if I would concern myself with petty office gossip. I thought you knew me better than that,” she answers, then sniffs. “But since even people in Legal, three floors down, know, then it’s safe to assume, that yes, everyone in the office knows.”

Kara’s not so sure how to feel about that.

“But that’s not really why I called you here,” Cat continues. “I have been informed that starting today—Scarlet, is it?” she asks, tilting her head towards Scarlet outside. Kara nods. “—that Scarlet will be your driver and bodyguard and it would be in my best interest not to let you run around National City doing errands. I have also been told that I will personally make sure that no reporter or paparazzi will ever come in contact with you while you are on CatCo premises, and that you are off-limits from our investigative team, no matter the outcome of this shit show farce Rhea started with your girlfriend.”

Kara’s jaw almost drops. She’s not quite sure she’s heard Cat Grant swear.

“Tell me this doesn’t have anything to do with her wayward spawn,” Cat drawls, her eyes rolling as if she already knows the answer.

“He was understandably upset when we broke up.” Kara’s not always so comfortable lying but she figures Cat would understand.

“Somehow I doubt that’s enough reason for him to cause this much trouble for Lena. The distasteful addition of the pictures and videos from long ago make it feel like it’s designed to cut deeper,” Cat says, looking at Kara like she’s reading her soul.

Well… she had been meaning to tell her boss, and she did promise Taja to tell her soon. No better time than the present, Kara thinks.

“There’s also the fact that I’m pregnant with his child, I guess…” Kara winces, dreading her boss’ reaction. Because, while she will not openly admit it, Cat Grant’s opinion matters to her, not just because she’s her boss, but because she’s grown to appreciate the woman behind the cold and perennially disappointed expression. She’s so much more than the tyrannical, ruthless businesswoman everyone paints her to be. Kara shakes her head. “It’s not like that… It’s just that you’ve been so buried and I didn’t want to disturb you so…”

Lena smiles at Kara and leans in for a quick kiss. “I give you permission to disturb me any time. I don’t care if I’m in the middle of a meeting, no matter how important it is.” Kara feels Lena’s warm hands cup her cheeks, “You two will always be more important, so make sure you remember that. Walk in on my meetings, call me anytime, bother Jess… I don’t care. If it’s you, it’s important, okay?”

Kara nods, her chest feeling tight in the best possible way.

“And you know, dinner with Taja is a great idea. Let’s see if we can ask to stay for the weekend there too…” Lena adds. “I don’t see how she’ll be able to say no to her favorite person.”

Kara laughs but abruptly stops when Lena shoves her phone on Kara’s hand. “I’m calling her?”

“Of course!” Lena says, grinning. “You didn’t actually think I’m her favorite person right now, did you?” Lena shakes her head. “Kara, she gave you Scarlet, the person she trusts most in this world, and her favorite car! She’s hosting Alex and Kelly, practically strangers, in her home. Think about what that means to a recluse like Taja.”

Kara frowns. “But won’t that be too much? Besides, I was kind of hoping to have you all to myself at least tomorrow.”

The wicked grin that Lena gives her sends a pleasant chill down her spine.

“Trust me, space and privacy won’t be a problem in Taja’s home. I’m almost disappointed you’ll get to see her place before you see the Luthor mansion.”

“That big, huh?”

Lena doesn’t answer, instead, she pulls Kara in for another kiss, this one hot, wet, and needy.

Which would explain why they are five minutes late for their appointment.

 

It does not, however, explain what Mike is doing there.

13. Chapter 13

“Before anyone says anything, I invited him in,” Taja says, her slight frame standing between Kara, Lena, and Mike. Curiously, however, her eyes are on Lena. A warning.

Lena assesses the situation, aware that Kara’s grip on her arm is the only thing that prevents Kara from exploding. To her credit, Kara, beyond tightening her hold on Lena’s arm, hasn’t said a word. She doesn’t need to. Her eyes, her demeanor, the way her shoulders are tensed… Lena’s all too familiar with it.

Lena doesn’t look at Mike. At least not at first. She glances around the familiar private room, noting Kelly and Alex on the l-shaped couch. Kelly looks wary while Alex is alert, her eyes darting to and from every person in the room. The older Danvers looking just slightly less unhappy than Kara.

Scarlet is at the door, tall and stony, eyes fixed on Mike, her expression unreadable.

And then there’s Taja who looks the calmest of all of them. She’s standing there, relaxed, seemingly unaware of the tension in the room.

But that’s Taja for you.

“I found him wandering around my lobby a few minutes before you arrived, asking for me. It was too late to send him away and I assumed you’d rather have an argument here than outside where there would be prying eyes and listening ears,” Taja continues, her voice soft, quiet, forcing everyone to focus and listen. “However, I am legally obliged to inform everyone in this room that under the law, everyone must obtain consent from the mother of the unborn child if they wish to be present for her doctor’s appointment and/or view her test results—everyone, including the father.”

Lena watches as Taja’s gaze finally turns to Kara, her eyes softening. “Kara Danvers?”

“I don’t want him here,” Kara says between gritted teeth. Lena slightly winces as Kara’s fingers dig deeper on her arm but she doesn’t pull away. In truth, there’s a part of Lena that’s missed this side of Kara but she’d rather not think about it now.

“That’s still my kid, so yeah, I have every right to be here,” Mike says taking a step forward. Before anyone else can make a move, Taja pivots to turn to him. She says nothing but the intent is clear. There’s a line Mike cannot cross.

“Oh now it’s your kid—” Kara starts but is instantly cut off.

“Mike, you’re not listening,” Lena says. “Taja literally just told you that you cannot be here without Kara’s express consent. And Kara just said ‘No.’”

Kara scoffs. “Consent.” It is said so viciously that everyone turns to Kara. “You hear that, Mike?” Kara’s grasp on Lena’s arm suddenly tightens to a degree that Lena couldn’t help the gasp and the wince that escapes from her. She clenches her fist in pain but makes no move to remove Kara’s hand. “Fucking consent…”

Lena’s head snaps to Kara, all discomfort on her arm now forgotten, only to be replaced by her heart dropping as ice fills her veins.

“This again,” Mike spits out. “It happened once and I already apolo—”

“When?” Lena asks, her eyes now on Mike. She feels Kara’s hold on her loosen.

“Lena…” She hears Kara from behind her, but everything sounds distant compared to the pounding in her ears.

“When, Mike?” she repeats.

Mike frowns, his eyes restless, darting everywhere except the space Lena occupies. “It doesn’t matter. I apologized.”

“Was it about fourteen weeks ago?” Lena can feel the twitch on her temple, a sign of an impending migraine, and she shivers slightly, everything inside her dropping a million degrees.

But as soon as she asks that question, several things happen at once.

Alex lunges towards Mike, fist clenched and raised, yelling “You fucking asshole!”

Kara takes a step forward to try and stop Alex but Lena puts her arm out, catching Kara and preventing her from moving forward.

Scarlet, a hair quicker than everyone, pushes Mike out of the way and instead catches Alex’s fist on her jaw. She shakes her head, straightens to her full height and stands between Alex and Mike.

This immediately calms everyone down.

“Oh my god, Scarlet.” Alex says, eyes wide with disbelief. She checks Scarlet’s face carefully before remembering what just happened. “What the hell were you thinking?” There’s a mix of concern and anger in her voice but Scarlet remains quiet, her head turned to Mike while her body is facing Alex.

Before anyone else can say or do anything though, Taja clears her throat.

“I believe Kara Danvers has made it clear that she does not consent to your presence here, Mr. Matthews,” Taja says, her voice unchanging, quiet, soft. “If you insist on staying, I will be forced to call security.” She pauses. “Or you can come with me and I’ll walk you out.”

Lena doesn’t miss the slight glance that Taja throws Kara’s way, before holding her hand out, gesturing towards the door.

“Kara—” Mike tries again but Taja cuts him off.

“Mr. Matthews. Please,” she insists as she walks to the door, holding it open.

It takes a second but Mike finally moves. Before he reaches the door, however, Lena steps in his way.

She looks at him, her eyes wandering over familiar features. She tries to remember the boy who was her friend but can’t quite find him anymore.

“I would have let this go on. I would have let you and Rhea continue with whatever you think you’re doing.”

She tries to remember the little boy that was just kind of around because his parents were there. He wasn’t perfect. He was entitled and spoiled but never cruel, which made him a million times better than most of the other kids in their circle. She remembers the young man who would tell her stories of all the women he hooked up with over the weekend. She remembers the times she’d had to pick him up because he woke up in the middle of nowhere with no money, no car, no phone.

“But now I’m done.” Lena pauses, letting the meaning sink in.

Mike finds the nerve to scoff. “You’re going to sic your lawyers on us?”

“Worse,” Lena says, her eyes still on Mike. “I’m actually going to start trying now.”

 

 

 

The door closes and there’s silence for a couple of seconds until Lena turns and pulls Kara to her, wrapping her in an embrace that she hopes would convey this overwhelming need she has to protect Kara, to shield her from every single thing that would harm her, to exhaust all her resources to make sure that nothing and no one hurts Kara ever again.

But right now, all she can do is hold Kara.

She feels Kara snake her arms around her waist and they stay that way for a bit until Kara murmurs, “I’m fine, Lena.”

Lena leans back to look at Kara.

“I wish you didn’t push him out of the way,” Alex says, her voice reminding both Kara and Lena that they weren’t alone.

Scarlet says nothing and when Lena turns to look at them, Scarlet has already resumed her place by the door while Alex and Kelly stand awkwardly, waiting for their turn to talk to Kara.

Lena places a gentle kiss on Kara’s temple before murmuring, “Go to Alex. I need to make a few phone calls.”

Kara passes her a look. It is fleeting but familiar and Lena smiles. “I’ll just be in that corner, over there.” She points to a far corner of the room, still visible from the medical bed and Kara nods.

 

 

 

 

Taja returns a few minutes later, alone, and they proceed with their appointment as usual.

Lena watches in the corner as Alex and Kelly ask Taja a million questions. She answers each one patiently while she checks on Kara. She can barely make out what they’re saying but that’s fine. The baby’s okay. Kara’s healthy. It’s all that really matters to Lena.

All her focus is on Kara right now.

And the deep, abiding anger that courses through her veins.

She spent a few minutes on a conference call with Jess and a few people, lining up meetings with key people and moving her schedule around to accommodate these additions.

She really was willing to just weather this through. It demanded a lot of adjustments, a lot of backtracking, a lot of late nights going through their numbers again and again, a lot of accommodating and compromise with her shareholders and board members, but she was more than happy to go through all of it. She deserves it. She understands that much.

She knows that between her and Kara, she had the power to shut their affair down. She could have stopped it. She could have backed off. She could have steered their relationship and remained friends, maybe found some willing body to fuck from time to time just to take the edge off.

But she didn’t.

Because while on paper, all that sounds true, the reality is that Lena never had much power when it comes to Kara. She never understood it either. She tried so hard to bury her feelings, and for a time, she really thought she did. But in truth the notion was laughable. Even when she tried to convince herself she felt nothing for Kara, the moment Kara would say her name, she was gone.

“Master Luthor.” A gentle voice pulls Lena’s attention, and she sees Scarlet holding an ice bag. “For your arm.”

Lena murmurs her thanks and watches as Scarlet bows slightly before walking away.

She holds the bag against her arm. She’s almost forgotten about it. But now that she remembers how hard Kara was holding back, it feeds her anger even more.

To her credit, Kara has been extremely patient in letting Lena deal with everything. She’s been nothing but supportive and has been going along with everything that’s been happening. She knows at one point or another, Kara’s going to push back. It’s only a matter of time.

But in light of what they just learned, everything has changed.

 

Their appointment with Taja ends and everyone decides to meet up again for dinner. Scarlet and Taja are more quiet than usual while Alex looks reluctant to Let Kara go. But Kelly reminds her that Lena and Kara still have to prepare for their weekend at Taja’s place.

Besides, it gives them a chance to talk, something she anticipates, knowing Kara.

She didn’t anticipate that the talk would start as soon as they drove away from Taja’s clinic.

 

 

 

 

“Say something?” Kara’s voice is small and distant and it raises Lena’s alarm bells. In response, she holds out a hand to gently touch Kara’s cheek. She manages to smile at Kara, an assurance, before turning her attention back to the road.

“Sorry, just a bit distracted,” Lena murmurs and it’s true. Between all the meetings and changes she suddenly has to juggle starting Monday, she’s also driving the two most precious beings in her life while dealing with the relentless pounding in her head.

“Do you want to talk about it?” Kara asks.

“Only if you’re comfortable.”

There’s a pause. Then, “Are you angry?”

Lena glances at Kara who seems to shrink back on her chair. But they promised not to lie so… “Yes, but not at you.” She sighs. “Why didn’t you tell me?”

There’s a long, deep inhale before, “I was already feeling so shitty about myself. I didn’t want you to pity me.”

Lena considers this for a moment. “I understand. But I don’t think of you less. You’re still my Kara. Nothing’s changed.”

There’s silence for a few seconds.

“You said you’re going to start trying now. What does that mean?” Kara asks.

Lena hesitates, her anger still pressing on her veins. Well, perhaps some things have changed.

“I’ve just been putting out fires. I haven’t really done anything to retaliate because I didn’t want this to get bigger than it needs to be.”

“Because you’ve been blaming yourself this whole time, haven’t you?”

Lena glances at Kara but doesn’t say anything. Because of course Kara can read her like a book.

“Yes,” she finally admits after a few seconds.

Kara’s answer, however, is swift and decisive.

“Park the car.”

Lena glances at Kara, brows raised. “We’re literally in the middle of—”

“Park. The fucking. Car,” Kara says, cutting her off. Her voice is even, low.

Lena’s first instinct is to fight back. But she’s too familiar with all the small nuances in the way Kara talks that she knows this is not the hill to die on.

“I’ll park it after the turn,” Lena concedes.

It takes a little over three minutes for Lena to find a decent parking spot and as soon as she pulls the handbrakes, Kara unlocks her seatbelt and pulls Lena in for a bruising kiss. Lena, anticipating a full-blown argument, is taken by surprise, but quickly adjusts. She knows her role when Kara’s being this aggressive. And to be fair, she’s missed it.

Except, she’d rather do this at home, not by the side of the road, out in public.

But Kara is demanding, relentless and Lena is weak to it all. She surrenders with a whimper and a sigh. And suddenly everything changes.

Kara slows down, becomes gentle, still demanding, her kisses still searing but it has lost its rough edges.

“You actual fucking idiot,” Kara murmurs against her lips when the need for air finally forces them to stop, or at least slow down. “You have a big brain, an even bigger heart but are still inexplicably dumb.”

Lena pulls back, brows furrowed. Kara looks at her with a strange mix of amusement and exasperation.

“You’re telling me the reason you’ve been working your ass off these past few weeks, the reason I’ve been missing out on our morning drives, the reason I’ve been waking up to find you passed out beside me, is because you think all of this is some karmic retribution? That you deserve to be punished?”

Kara pulls her in again, this time her kisses are slow, sweet, too gentle. When she pulls away to look at Lena, her eyes are expectant.

Lena sighs and leans back on her seat. “Imagine you never met me, that I never pursued any sort of relationship with you, that I never invited you to lunch, that I just signed those documents you delivered to my office and sent you on your way.”

Kara is quiet, her eyes on Lena’s, her expression unreadable.

“You would’ve had a quiet life, probably found someone less complicated, and wouldn’t be stuck in the middle of this war Rhea has decided to initiate.”

Lena looks ahead, unable to bear the weight of Kara’s stare any longer. She looks down the road and sees a family and their dog. “You would be safe, hopefully happy, stable.”

“I’d be miserable,” Kara says. Lena turns to her and finds that Kara is also looking at the family. “I wouldn’t find a reason to want to be better. I’d never have a chance to go back to college. I’d probably be swearing off dating, only to latch on to the first person who seems to like me at the very least. That’s not what being safe, happy or stable is, Lena.” Kara looks back to Lena. “Is this your way of telling me you’re regretting ever meeting me?”

Lena glances at Kara, then back to the family and their dog. They’re laughing. The dog walking proudly beside the wife while the husband carries their baby on his shoulder. She can see the wife gently berating the husband. Probably because that’s really unsafe. But the baby looks like he’s enjoying himself, grabbing at his father’s hair.

“We should get a dog,” Lena murmurs. “Personally, I prefer cats, but I get the feeling you prefer dogs.” She sighs, then turns to Kara, taking her hand, squeezing it lightly. “I don’t regret meeting you. I regret being afraid of my own feelings, of second-guessing myself, and dragging you into this mess. And that’s always going to be my fault…”

She lets go of Kara’s hand and gently touches Kara’s stomach. Something resets inside Lena.

“But I’m done putting out fires.” She looks at Kara and she feels ice fill her veins again. “We’re going to spend a happy weekend with our family and friends—”

“And you’re going to start setting fires on Monday…” Kara whispers, her voice trembling.

Lena sits up and starts the car.

“Long before our Little Hatchling is born, people will remember the Matthews only as a cautionary tale.”

 

 

 

Kara has seen Lena angry.

Once.

Okay, twice, if she counts that time Rhea started messing with her company.

It’s such a rare occurrence that it’s jarring to see.

Lena’s not the warm, ray-of-sunshine kind of person. She’s serious most of the time with a steely disposition, so Kara has learned to treasure their time together when Lena smiles, her shoulders are relaxed and she can let herself slouch and be carefree.

On a normal day, Lena is just… Lena. Serious, curious, observant, and passionate about pretty much anything she gets her hands on. There’s fire in her eyes, solid and steady, animated by what Kara imagines is the hundreds of thoughts and ideas always floating around in her head. While people think of Kara, on a good day, as the literal embodiment of the sun, Kara likes to think that Lena is a whole constellation of stars. A magnificent splash of twinkling lights that you can’t help but look up to at night. The darker the night, the brighter they shine. But once you manage to bridge the distance, you discover that these twinkling, cold lights are fiery giants, their lights far reaching, hope-giving.

But the first time Kara saw Lena angry, the difference was heaven and hell. Her posture was stiff, her eyes cold, her voice so finely controlled and yet Kara felt the raging waters batting against an insurmountable dam. She wasn’t a raging ball of fire and light. No, Lena felt like a collapsed star, a black hole capable of dismantling anything and everything that decides to get in its way.

Except that Lena isn’t just one star. Lena, when angry, feels like a whole constellation of black stars, with Kara being the only one immune to her roiling power.

And try as she might now, Lena cannot hide her anger from Kara. She suspects Lena’s not even trying. She’s been angry since Kara inadvertently revealed what Mike did.

Kara’s not about to pretend that what happened didn’t affect her, that she’s over it. She’s not. She probably never will be. She will always carry it with her, either as a festering wound, or a scar… depending on how therapy goes.

But the day after, when she was feeling so shitty, she came to Lena’s, intending to unload everything. Except, Lena was happy that day. She was animated, smiling, bright and almost buzzing. Something happened with a project or something and for once everything was going right with Lena’s day. She called it perfect.

“And now that you’re here, it’s even more perfect!” Lena had said as she handed Kara a glass of wine.

So Kara drank the wine and celebrated and by the end of the night, she was feeling just a little bit better. Lena always made her feel better, no matter how shitty her day has been. And she wanted Lena to stay happy. She didn’t want to ruin her day.

Besides, if she was going to be honest, she really didn’t know how to open up to Lena about it. She was so afraid that Lena would look at her differently, like something to be pitied…

But today proves just how wrong she was.

Lena, with all the anger coursing through her veins, still manages to be soft and gentle when it comes to Kara. Nothing changed, even now when they’re supposed to be packing their things for the weekend but ended up making out and grinding on each other on the sofa, Lena’s touches are sure, hungry, and setting fire to Kara’s soul.

Nothing’s changed in their relationship.

Well, one thing has.

 

 

 

“Is Lena okay?” Kelly asks.

They are in the gardens of Taja’s… property, watching the rising tide.

She’s not sure what to call it, actually.  She just remembers Lena turning a corner and telling Kara that all the land starting from that point onward is Taja’s. They drove for a good forty-five minutes through winding mountain roads, Lena being too careful, before she even saw the gate to Taja’s main grounds.

As they drove, Lena gave her the rundown of Taja’s property.

“The whole thing is spread out so that it encompasses a mountain and the surrounding valleys. It also has parts of a major river system running through it. Taja’s main grounds has a sea inlet. She also owns a few of the islands visible from her manor.

“If we take this other turn, we’ll come across a village, complete with its own city center. It houses the forest rangers, utility workers, farmers, shop owners, groundskeepers, a handful of veterinarians, doctors, nurses, the police stationed here, all of them, except the cops, employed by the Cunningham estate.”

Kara frowned in thought as she turned to Lena. “I hope it’s not too rude to ask, but—and I know the Cunninghams are rich—but, you can’t tell me the Cunningham estate is shouldering the entire cost for the upkeep of this. It’s massive.”

Lena smirked. “When National City was going through its growing pains and developers were grabbing land left and right, the Cunninghams basically stopped them from moving further by securing this area. I’m not sure which of the old Cunninghams it was, but one of them was a conservationist and pushed to buy the wild spaces and the sensitive coastline habitats for the animals living here. This was on top of the Cunninghams making National City their main hub for business, so to speak. Let’s just say that National City is deeply incentivized to keep the Cunninghams living and dealing within its borders. So, they offered them major tax cuts. All that money is then used to preserve this land and pay and feed their workers.”

Kara whistled but was immediately cut off when Lena took a turn.

The road emerged from wooded mountains to an open vista and suddenly there’s a whole ocean in front of them. Its waters twinkling blue and orange as the sun gradually lowered.

“If you look down,” Lena had said, her eyes on the road. “You’ll see a midcentury modern mansion with a heli pad and a hangar. That’s Taja’s.”

Kara peered down and saw in the distance, at the base of the mountain, facing a sea inlet and indeed with a visible heli pad and a half-cylinder shaped structure that Kara supposed was the hangar, what looked like geometric shapes, conforming to the landscape, connected and yet spread out. Even from a distance, she can see the glasses reflecting back sunlight.

“I should have known,” Kara muttered under her breath.

“What was that?” Lena asked, her eyes still on the road. She had never seen Lena drive this carefully before.

“I was just thinking,” Kara said, “I shouldn’t have expected a typical mansion for Taja. I was expecting something like a Tudor or some French Provincial type of mansion. Or maybe even a castle. But… midcentury modern wasn’t on my list.”

“It only makes sense,” Lena answered. “It was built in the fifties. And besides, the style seamlessly integrates with the natural landscape, which would have made the conservationist Cunningham pretty happy. Then you have the typical large windows, wide, open spaces, and since it’s a seaside property, it can’t rise too high, otherwise, it’ll get battered by storm winds. So, this is perfect.”

“Taja told you all this?”

Lena scoffed. “No. Actually, it was Rhea who told me.”

Kara’s brows furrowed. “Why would Rhea know all that?”

Lena hesitated, drumming her fingers on the steering wheel, before answering. “Remember that thing that I said was not my or Taja’s story to tell?” Kara nodded. “It’s part of it. But I suppose you should know this part, at least. Rhea and John, Taja’s father, used to be close. Pretty sure they went to the same boarding school and graduated from the same university. Beyond that, only Alice and Rhea can tell you.”

Kara shrugged. By then, they had wound their way down the mountain and were entering a heavy-looking, automatic wrought iron gate. It had a scanner on the driver’s side where Lena waved a black card.

They drove down a tree-lined gravel driveway that ended in a small parking space where Scarlet was waiting for them.

And Kara was sure Lena downplayed the part about privacy and space not being a problem in Taja’s mansion because Scarlet ushered them to an entire section of the mansion specifically designated for them.

“I got it from here, Scarlet,” Lena said, gesturing Kara down a hallway with thick glass walls on both sides, showing on one side a mini garden, and the other the ocean. “This is where I usually stay when I’m here. The mansion was built to house families. Plural.”

Their section has a master bedroom with a balcony overlooking the gardens and ocean, two relatively smaller guest rooms (though they’re at least twice the size of Kara’s bedroom), ensuite bathrooms, a living area, a bar, a breakfast nook, a gym, and a small library.

“It’s like having our own house,” Kara remarked.

“We can be as loud as we want here,” Lena’s voice was quiet, low, and promising a world of pleasure as she snaked her arms around Kara’s waist. “And no one in the mansion will hear us.”

Kara smirked. “I like that.”

That was the plan, of course. But Kara and Lena really should have known better because they hadn’t even began unpacking their essentials when Kelly burst into their room, her smile wide and bright.

“You have to see the garden!”

And that’s how Kara found herself lounging about on one of the outdoor sofas, watching the tide rise from a safe distance.

 

 

“Kara?”

Kelly’s voice brings her back to the present.

“Sorry, what was that?”

Kelly’s smile is patient. “I asked if Lena is okay.”

Kara looks around, then at Kelly. “Nothing escapes you.”

“That’s not true. She just feels different.”

“What do you mean?” Kara looks at Kelly who hesitates, drinks an entire flute of mimosa, before answering.

“She just… She feels inaccessible, you know what I mean? She never felt that way before.”

Kara sighs. “She’s angry. She tries not to show it, but she’s furious.”

“Is it because of…” Kelly tapers off and Kara understands. Even if it is Kelly’s job to ask these questions, it can’t be easy asking someone who’s family.

“Yes,” Kara answers evenly, reaching for one of the sliced apples served in front of them. “I think it just finally pushed her over the edge, you know?”

“What about you? How are you?”

Kara looks at Kelly. “Honestly? Relieved,” she answers. “It’s the one thing I’ve been hiding from Lena—from everyone—and I feel like a weight has been lifted.” She sighs. “But also just a confused mix of everything. I’m excited for our baby, anxious about what’s happening with Lena and Rhea… I feel like everything is on a knife’s edge.”

Kelly takes her hand. “You’re far stronger than you think you are. I hope you know that.”

Kara smiles at her. “I’m glad Alex met you.”

Kelly pulls her in for a hug and Kara allows herself to relax, to sink into it. They separate in time for Scarlet to arrive, holding a tray with a pitcher of water and a couple of glasses with ice on them. As soon as she’s laid down the contents of the tray, she stands to the side. Kelly frowns.

“Scarlet? I thought you were supposed to teach them how to cook?” she asks. Kara turns to Scarlet, waiting for her reply.

“The Master decided that they are three, adult women, capable of reading and following instructions,” Scarlet answers with a slight bow of her head. She straightens up and looks over the ocean.

“And Lena agreed to this?” Kara asks.

“Yes, Master Danvers.”

Kara looks at Kelly before turning to Scarlet again. “Aren’t you worried they’ll burn down your kitchen?”

“I am.” She takes a tentative glance towards where Kara thinks the kitchen might be before returning her gaze back to the ocean spread before them.

Kara pats the space between her and Kelly. “Come, sit with us. You’re too nervous.”

Kelly laughs and moves, freeing some more space for Scarlet who looks hesitant for a moment before taking the proffered seat.

“So, Scarlet…” Kelly starts, trying hard not to grin. “What’s this I hear about you losing a bet?”

Scarlet, who already looks so out of place, with her long legs, pristine white tux, and stiff posture, suddenly looks uncomfortable. She makes a small noise that Kara thinks might be her clearing her throat.

“It was a long time ago,” Scarlet says, her voice lower than usual. “My parents had just died and I was sick and the doctors couldn’t figure out what was wrong with me.”

Something about Scarlet’s awkward demeanor changes. “But you’ve seen how the Master is. She came to my bed and refused to leave. She told me that if I survive, I would have to call her Master. We made a bet. She won.”

Kara gets the feeling that there’s more to it than that, but Scarlet is already being more generous with her personal information than ever before, so she lets it go.

Kelly laughs. “She really can be full of herself.”

Scarlet nods and smiles a small, fond smile. “But it worked, almost as if she healed me by her sheer stubbornness alone.”

“Has she always been headstrong?” Kelly asks.

“She has no choice.”

“They never do,” Kara says softly, her eyes on her hands, neatly folded on her lap.

 

 

 

Dinner is surprisingly palatable.

The chicken is slightly dry. The rice is a hair undercooked. And if it was up to Kara, the eggplant would have had a bit more salt.

But it wasn’t the disaster they all thought it would be.

They spent the rest of the evening teaching Taja and Scarlet how to graciously lose at Monopoly. Graciously because Alex and Lena are the worst losers Kara has ever met, especially when both of them are drunk.

And it wasn’t as if either of them were winning. Kara was. She won all three rounds, but Alex and Lena were both so set on destroying each other that they hardly noticed it. When they did it was only because one of them was accusing the other of helping Kara win.

At least, Kelly noted, she now has Scarlet to help her keep the peace. Scarlet merely nodded while Taja watched the entire exchange curiously.

 

That night, Kara and Lena didn’t manage to reach their bedroom before they were both naked and Lena was panting Kara’s name as she was brought from one orgasm to the next.

They barely made it to their bedroom.

 

 

“Oh god, you’re naked!”

Kara winces awake while Lena hums, burrowing deeper into Kara.

“You’ve seen me naked, Alex,” Kara mutters. She doesn’t have to look at Alex to know that Alex is probably shielding her eyes.

“Not you. I was talking about Len—oh god. My eyes!”

“Fuck you, Alex. I look good naked,” Lena says, not even bothering to pull up the sheets as she wraps her arms tighter around Kara’s waist.

“Yes, you do,” Kara mutters as she places a kiss on top of Lena’s head.

“Well, I’m not in love with you so get dressed. Taja and Scarlet are going to drive us around today. Breakfast is also ready. We leave in thirty minutes,” Alex hurriedly says, slamming the double doors of the wing’s master bedroom.

Lena groans. “I don’t know why she was expecting us to be dressed in bed. Can you imagine going to bed dressed?”

Kara can almost hear Lena grinning. “I’ve forgotten what that’s like,” Kara says.

“Oh that’s right, you used to have boring sex.”

Kara hums. “No, I thought I had okay sex.”

“Then you met me.”

“Wow, the ego on you,” Kara teases but pulls Lena in closer, placing another soft kiss on top of her head.

“It’s not ego if it’s true.”

Kara really couldn’t say anything to counter that. Instead, she tilts Lena’s head up and kisses her, long and deep.

They come out of their wing an hour later, much to Alex’s annoyance and to Scarlet and Taja’s amusement. Kelly shakes her head.

 

 

The rest of the day goes pleasantly. Scarlet drives them around while Taja acts like a subdued version of those tour guides in buses with microphones, that is, until Alex calls her out.

“Please don’t tell me you wrote a script for this,” Alex says, grinning, staring pointedly at Taja’s phone.

They are in one of Taja’s luxury 4WD’s, Kelly and Kara in the middle while Lena and Alex were shunted to the very back to carry on their grumbling about last night and this morning. The drive takes them along a scenic route, and Kara can see a couple of similar vehicles trailing them in the distance, almost invisible sometimes.

“I—I did not. It’s just bullet points,” Taja answers but quickly pockets her phone. “In truth, I haven’t done this before.”

Alex’s grin disappears. “What do you mean? You’ve never toured your own property before?”

“I have. Several times. I make it a point to do it at least twice a year with some of the rangers,” Taja says. “I’ve just… I never had people who would spend a weekend with me without really expecting anything from me.”

“You mean, friends?” Alex asks, prompting an almost inaudible groan from Kelly and Lena clearing her throat. Kara just stares at Taja’s profile.

“Yes.”

“Oh.”

“Uhmm. But you have Lena…” Alex sputters.

“Lena Luthor is the closest thing to a sister I will ever have,” Taja says, nodding. “But we both have busy schedules and she’s seen most of this before.”

Lena nods but doesn’t say anything.

“Well…uhmm…” Alex clears her throat. “Since we’re friends, you’re now obligated to come join us in Midvale for Thanksgiving, maybe even the Holidays if you can clear you calendar. I’m sure Eliza would love to meet you.”

“Why didn’t Eliza come with you?” Lena asks Alex.

Alex shrugs. “I’m not really sure. She was supposed to, but apparently their company is downsizing so she’s doing more these days. She sacrificed having an assistant and a few more staff just to keep her department and her lab running.”

“But their company was doing well at the start of the year. They just got additional funds from their merger with…” Lena tapers off and it causes Kara to turn around and look at her, the glint in Lena’s eyes familiar. She looks at Taja for confirmation and sees the same knowing look in the other heiress’s eyes.

“…You can sleep in Kara’s old room,” Alex says, the tail end of a sentence Kara didn’t hear. But it’s enough to get everyone’s attention.

“Wait, what?” Kara asks, suddenly turning to Alex. “I don’t want to sleep in your old bed. It’s got cooties.”

“Don’t be gross, Kara,” Alex says. “And it doesn’t have cooties. I practice safe sex.”

“You mean boring,” Lena quips, earning her a glare from both Alex and Kelly.

“Excuse me!?” Alex starts, indignant, her volume rising.

“What’s cooties?”

Everyone turns to Taja who is looking at all of them through the rearview mirror. It takes a second, but eventually everyone breaks out into laughter.

It is soft, gentle, typical of Taja, but she turns to Scarlet and, in the middle of everyone’s laughter, Kara hears her say, “I’ve never been laughed at to my face before.”

Taja smiles before she starts softly laughing too.

 

 

 

Kara is under no illusion that that weekend was just the calm before the storm, if Lena’s mood is anything to go by.

She was at least happy that it went so well with Taja agreeing to spend Thanksgiving and Christmas break in Midvale. Lena and Kara left shortly after the jet carrying Kelly and Alex back to Midvale took off. Their drive home was quiet, uneventful, and Kara slept part of the way, apologizing when she woke up as Lena is parking.

“It’s okay,” Lena says with a soft smile on her face. “It was a pretty eventful day and you didn’t get much sleep last night.” Kara wants to say Lena didn’t get much sleep either but she’s still a little too sleepy for that.

Instead, she asks, “Where are we?” looking around.

“Noonan’s. I called ahead while you were sleeping to order some snacks in case you get hungry later and it’s too late to cook or order out,” Lena answers. “Stay here while I pick it up.”

Kara nods, blinking, her brain still not fully awake.

A few seconds after Lena exits the car, Kara’s phone rings, Cat Grant’s name flashing on the screen.

Kara frowns. Her boss hasn’t called her during the weekend in such a long time.

“Ms. Grant?”

“Kara? Did you check your e-mail?” Kara’s familiar with that tone and it gets her heart racing, her anxiety building.

“No. I was sleeping. I’ll check it now. I’ll put you on speaker.”

“Before you do, I need you to understand that I only managed to get this copy in advance from someone who used to work with me.”

“What--?”

Kara sees the e-mail notification and quickly opens it.

It’s a forwarded e-mail from her boss containing portion of an article from the National City Post, a tabloid, and not even a good one.

She reads the e-mail. Then reads it again. And again. Each time understanding it less and less.

“Ms. Grant…” Kara says. “We can’t let Lena or Taja know about this.”
