She feels a familiar, cold, sticky feeling all over her skin as she walks down the long, carpeted path towards Cat Grant’s office. Her heart is loud, louder and heavier with every step she has to take, but she manages to keep the rush of memories at bay. In this moment, nothing else exists in Kara’s world but this path. The rest of CatCo could be in chaos and she wouldn’t have noticed it.

For the first time in a very long time, Cat Grant is there before Kara. She can see her through the glass walls of her office, pacing barefoot, talking to someone on the phone. When Cat sees her, she pauses, and Kara notices the slight change in her expression before it snaps back to its previous state.

She knows that expression all too well.

Cat gestures at her, and Kara picks up her pace. She is almost startled by the presence of a tall figure walking past her. Scarlet, ever perceptive, is there, holding Cat Grant’s door open for Kara, her head slightly bowed.

Kara murmurs her thanks and waits. In her periphery, she can see Scarlet dropping her things, arranging them on her desk and pulling up the chair that has now been assigned to her.

“I’m disappointed, Jim,” Cat says on her phone. Her voice brings Kara’s attention back to her boss. She’s familiar with that tone. It’s a variation of what she hears Lena do sometimes when she’s on a call. It not only conveys the magnitude of her disappointment, but the severity of the consequences if nothing about the situation changes. Kara has only ever been the recipient of that tone from Cat Grant once and never again.

Cat sighs as she ends the call before carefully replacing her phone on her desk.

“I tried my best to get it scrubbed,” Cat says, rubbing her forehead as she sits on the sofa. “Sit,” she adds, gesturing to the couch in front of her.

Kara, numb, walks forward and sits across from her boss.

“It’s not as ‘blind’ as blind items go but I have to ask,” Cat says, her eyes staring intently at Kara, “Why are you trying to keep it from Lena and Taja? You know that they will most likely find out about this within the day, if they haven’t already.”

Kara fidgets, grateful for her boss’ newfound patience as she finds her words.

“Lena...,” Kara starts, head down, voice trembling. “You know how private she is,” she says vaguely gesturing towards Scarlet who is now sitting at her station, reading a book. “And Taja is a million times worse.” Kara sighs. “We’re not exactly trying to hide our relationship, I think, but a measure of privacy is something that Lena deserves, at least.”

There’s a beat, a pause, and Kara sees Cat Grant narrow her eyes at her. “And it has nothing to do with the fact that you still think you don’t deserve to be seen, let alone be known as Lena’s significant other?”

“I don’t see why it matters,” Kara says. She can hear her blood rushing, feel the clamminess of her skin. She looks at the door, knowing exactly how many steps she has to take. “Whatever I am worth is entirely eclipsed by Lena. She’s the Inventor, the Engineer, the Prodigy. I won’t ever be able to stand on equal grounds with her.”

“No one will,” Cat answers, her tone quiet, eyes intent on Kara.

“Exactly,” Kara’s tone even quieter.

“It still doesn’t answer my question.” Cat’s eyes are boring into her as if she’s truly seeing her assistant for the very first time. “We agree. Lena has earned her place on an incredibly high pedestal.” There’s a pause that feels like an opening chasm but lasts for only a moment. “But she came down from her throne and stole my assistant from me.” Another pause. Kara sees her daintily dust an invisible fleck of something from her lap. “And it looks like she intends to keep her.”

Kara doesn’t respond. She’s not really sure what to say now.

“The article,” Cat continues, “It mentions something of a troubled past?”

That cold, clammy feeling returns in full, and Kara feels bile rise to the back of her throat. She blinks and for a split second, she is on a carpeted aisle, flanked on either side by pews. At the end of the aisle are two long, horizontal, wooden cases with rounded tops. She never gets to the end of the aisle. She blinks again.

“My parents died when I was young,” Kara says. “Car accident.” She can feel sweat running down her spine. “Bounced around from one foster home to another until they found my cousin.”

“The one in Metropolis?” She barely hears her boss’ question but Kara nods.

“He tried, I think,” Kara says, hating the fact that she must be the one to justify everything. “He was young, just landed his first job in the big city and wasn’t ready for the added responsibility of one more mouth to feed.”

What little trust Kara had left, whittled away by adults and a system that was supposed to take care of an orphan child, he undermined completely when he almost immediately gave up on her and handed her to the Danvers.

She’s almost forgotten about him too. “But I’m grateful for him, in a way. I never would’ve met the Danvers if it weren’t for him.”

“Exaggeration in a blind item. Imagine that.” Cat says dryly before her expression softens. “I’m sorry about your parents.”

Kara nods numbly. “There’s really not a lot of good stories that come out of the system, so maybe they’re referring to that?”

“Sure,” Cat responds with an audible eye roll. She sighs. “By now, I’m certain people have figured out who that article is referring to. I can protect you while you’re in this building. I suppose I can trust that your sentinel over there is not as oblivious as she looks.”

Kara follows Cat’s eyeline and finds at least three people glancing surreptitiously at Scarlet who seems unaware of the attention, her focus on her book alone.

But Kara knows better. Scarlet’s various eccentricities—her vertical aesthetic, the hard-to-miss pristine, white tuxedo, and the perpetual blank stare—belie her attention to detail and the speed to which she can react to stimulus. “I trust her,” she murmurs.

Cat nods, stands up and heads over to her desk. “And what about the insinuation that you’re just using Lena because your quote-unquote ‘rich baby daddy’ left you?”  

Kara shakes her head, twisting her fingers. “It’s somewhat true, I guess. I didn’t set out for it to be this way, but it happened and I don’t know if I would believe my own story if I ever read it.”

Cat purses her lips and is quiet for ten agonizing, very loud seconds.

“Would you like an early maternity leave then?” she asks.

“No,” Kara answers, her voice shaky. “If I accept your offer, the temptation will always be there to just disappear. I think, for once, I’d like to weather this through.”

“But you have to admit, it would make it easier for Lena to protect you,” Cat says with a slight tilt of her head.

Kara stands before taking another deep breath. “Yes, but it would also make it easier for me to spiral out of control. Lena has assured me more than enough times that she’s going to be there when I need her. I want her to know I trust her.”

“You didn’t trust her enough when you tried to keep this article from being published though.” There is no hint of accusation in Cat Grant’s voice.

“I think…” Kara twists her fingers. “I panicked.”

“I understand.”

Does she?

Kara’s thoughts are interrupted by the loud, vibrating noise of Cat Grant’s phone. Cat sighs. “The offer stands,” she says before picking her phone up and dismissing Kara with a wave.

Before she leaves, she hears Cat mutter to her phone, “Jim…”

 

 

 

 

The day gets progressively worse, but Kara anticipated that much.

She is almost unable to do her job, her concentration continuously being broken by the incessant beeping of the receptionists’ phones. Just by the repetitive glances from the receptionists at their station, she can tell that they’re fielding a lot of calls asking for or inquiring about her. She makes a mental note to bring them donuts and coffee tomorrow.

Those who already dislike her for some reason, mostly for “breaking Mike’s heart” have just been given additional fuel for their disdain. She suddenly finds her prints missing or her access to the copier blocked so that she has to go down the hall and into another department to make copies. The calls that do make it past the receptionist desk, or those that have Kara’s extension number, are exceedingly friendly, entirely useless, and almost always devolve into a discussion that circles around Lena Luthor. It’s mildly infuriating at the beginning, but as the day wears on it begins to grow intolerable to the point where she handles tasks that could be accomplished with a simple phone call by sending emails instead. It makes everything slower, which just adds to her frustration.

The only bright spot of her day is when Lena sends her a brief message at lunch, letting Kara know that she’s coming home late. Despite the circumstances, Kara smiles down at her phone.

Lena doesn’t need to message her. She’d already set proper expectations earlier, but Kara appreciates them anyway. She knows how busy Lena is right now, how little time she has for herself, and the fact that—

Her phone starts to ring and the smile that has started to fade immediately returns.

“Hey,” Kara says, trying hard to school her face.

“You sound tired,” Lena says, then, “I have a minute, so I thought I’d let you know I love you.”

Kara’s heart, despite the day she’s been having so far, flutters. “I love you too,” she mutters under her breath. “And yeah, it’s been a day,” she sighs, picking at her food. Beside her, Scarlet is unwrapping a sandwich, neatly cut diagonally.

“Tell me about it later?”

Kara smirks. “I can think of a few better things to do than talking about my day.”

“Hmm. That bad?”

“Nothing I can’t handle,” Kara replies.

There’s a pause before Lena replies. “Good. I’ll try to get out of my meeting early tonight. I’ll see you at home.”

“Okay. I love you.”

“I love you too.”

She holds her phone to her ear a few seconds, waiting, maybe hoping, before she puts it down.

 

 

Sometime in the afternoon, Cat Grant calls Scarlet into her office where they speak for literally fifteen seconds. When Kara inquires, Scarlet simply shrugs and quietly says, “Parking issues. It’s all sorted now.”

Kara spends an extra couple of hours after work playing catch up. She finds she’s able to do more now that fewer people are watching her. And at the end of the day, instead of their usual parking spot in front of CatCo, Kara finds herself in CatCo’s parking deck, in the level set aside for VIPs and guests. Beside their car is an empty spot labelled, “CEO – CatCo Ent.”

“It’s a bit further than where we usually park, but it’s more secure, more private,” Scarlet says, anticipating, perhaps, the question Kara is not about to ask.

“Does Taja know?” Kara asks instead.

Scarlet doesn’t answer.

She doesn’t need to.

 

 

Lena comes home past one in the morning and maybe Kara is just imagining it, but Lena holds her just a little bit tighter than usual.

 

 

The next few weeks wear on and, truth be told, it really isn’t as terrible as Kara anticipated it would be.

Maybe because she has Scarlet who manages to keep away photographers and who suddenly developed the keen sense of knowing whenever Kara visits a website, internet forum, or article that has anything to do with her and Lena, then taking over her mouse and exiting said websites. Or maybe it’s because Cat Grant loudly and openly fired an intern who was trying to sneakily take a picture of Kara, an obvious warning to everyone.

Despite Scarlet’s best efforts however, Kara has, of course, seen some of the internet discourse surrounding her and she’s mildly surprised that, apart from a few nasty comments, most of it is rather supportive. Lauding her for her “hustle” and the fact that she is seemingly able to do what no other man or woman has been able to do: get The Lena Luthor to commit to a relationship.

The only thing that’s truly making everything terrible is the unending whispers, the glances, the looks of judgment and disapproval from people who only ever saw one side of the story. She admits that she helped the narrative that Mike created while he worked there: that he was a doting partner and a charming, if slightly dopey, co-worker. Kara gets the sense that if she starts explaining now, she’ll just bury herself deeper in this already toxic sludge that Mike left her in, that she can’t seem to get out of, so she prefers to stay quiet.

She’s only too glad that she can still rely on James and Nia, who now drop by her station more frequently than they used to just to check up on her. They talk about literally anything but Mike, Lena, or her pregnancy, choosing instead to talk about themselves, complain about co-workers, or about how someone else should plan Game Night so it doesn’t have to always be Kara’s responsibility.

The closest they ever come to discussing Lena is when Nia asks about Scarlet, who merely glances at her.

“She was recommended by a close family friend. I’ll tell you more later,” Kara answers. Nia narrows her eyes at Scarlet but nods and changes topic.

 

 

If Lena or Taja know about the article, they don’t mention it. Kara doesn’t ask.

And while Lena is pretty good at seeming oblivious to what Kara’s trying to hide, she cannot hide her exhaustion. Kara hears it in the groan of utter relief that escapes Lena’s chest when she crawls onto their bed and into Kara’s arms. She also cannot hide the lines and the bags under her eyes when she removes her armor.

And really, at this point, Kara thinks Lena’s not even trying to hide it. There’s no reason to hide it. Lena isn’t perfect. She doesn’t have to be. Not when she’s with Kara … which makes Kara feel even guiltier.

To somehow make up for it, Kara has forbidden Lena from discussing anything work-related at home, especially on Sundays, Lena’s only rest day. While Lena still spends most of the morning sending out emails, she makes it a point to put her phone away starting at lunch. They usually spend Sunday afternoons cuddling and then going out to some private restaurant in the evening only for both of them to fall into a food coma by the time they get home.

 

 

 

 

“How long was I supposed to wait before you tell me about that article?” Alex whisper-shouts into her phone a week later. Kara can tell by her voice that Alex is in her protective stance, hands on hips, eyebrow raised.

“I was hoping to not have to tell you, to be honest,” Kara says softly. She looks around. The day after the article came out, Scarlet stopped reading her Kindle and instead began the habit of staring down anyone who even remotely looked at Kara. It’s insanely effective. Now, Kara can use her mobile phone without worrying about people trying to decipher who she’s talking to.

“Did you think this would just die off without me noticing?”

“That was the hope.”

A sigh. “It didn’t, and now I’m sitting in my office reading about this nonsense.”

“It really is all nonsense, Alex. I don’t even know why people bother. So, the great Lena Luthor is dating a pregnant woman. Great. Let’s move on.”

“They’re heavily implying that you’re just doing this for the money, Kara.”

“They can imply all they want, Alex. As long as you, mom and Lena know the truth, the world can fuck off.”

There’s a brief pause on the other end of the line before Kara hears a slight chuckle. “That’s right. But even if you were just doing this for the money—which I know you’re not—I hope you know I love you.”

Something grips at Kara’s chest. It isn’t unpleasant, more like a reminder that her heart is there.

“Oh. By the way, Mom’s been pestering me since yesterday to get in contact with Lena but I was so busy. And the times I did try, she was out of office or in a meeting.”

Kara furrows her brow. “What’s up?”

“Didn’t Lena tell you? She bought majority shares in Mom’s company, injected some more money into it, so now Mom can rehire her lab assistants and buy some needed upgrades or something.”

“No, we don’t really talk shop at home.” Or have time, really. But that’s between her and Lena.

“Yeah well, Mom’s been trying to figure out how to thank her personally, but she’s got gatekeepers and Jess told me her calendar is full until the end of time.”

“Okay, I’ll let Lena know tonight.”

“Preferably with clothes on?”

“Don’t be gross, Alex.”

“I love you too, Kara.” Pause. “Listen, I gotta go, but please don’t hesitate to call me whenever, okay? If you need me to slap someone, I will. Just like in high school. Okay, bye.”

Kara shakes her head as she puts her phone away.

“Is everything okay?”

She turns around to find Scarlet staring at her.

“Yeah. It’s just Alex being Alex.”

There’s a slight pause, something like hesitation, “Are they okay?”

Kara smiles. “They’re fine. She’s just a bit mad that I didn’t tell her about the article sooner.”

Scarlet nods but doesn’t say anything more for the rest of the day.

 

 

 

 

A couple of weeks later, in the middle of Wednesday, she receives an extremely apologetic call from Lena saying that she has to fly out to Germany until Friday and that she’s sorry it’s all so last minute.

“It’s okay, Lena,” Kara says as confidently as she can, she can feel cold sweat starting to run down her spine.

“I’ll update you when I land and call you when I’m at the hotel.”

“Stop worrying about me,” Kara says, filling the gaps.

“I can’t help it…” Lena says, quietly. “You realize this is the first time I won’t be sleeping beside you since you moved in?”

“You sound like you’re in love.”

“It’s terrible. The thought of not crawling to bed with you starfished all over it and waking up without half your body on top of me.”

“That bad, huh?”

“I’m having a body pillow with your scent and face made as we speak.”

“I have a scent?”

“Yes. Pencil shavings, decaf, and that sweet cologne you love so much, I’m tempted to buy the company that makes it so no one else can ever wear it… It does things to me.”

“I need a detailed report about said things.”

She hears Lena chuckle, a deep, throaty sound that fills her being. “I’ll have it in your inbox by tomorrow morning, your time.”

Kara laughs. “I love you.”

“I love you too. I’ll make it up to you this weekend. I promise.”

“No,” Kara says, shaking her head. “You know you don’t have to.”

“I know. I want to. Kara… I want to.”

There’s a pause between them both before Lena, with an almost audible smile says, “Too much?”

“Never enough.”

There’s a soft groan on the other line. “When you say things like that…”

“I know. I know. I’ll behave,” Kara says, laughing.

“I have to go. I’ll keep you updated. Or Jess will. I love you.”

Kara is just about able to say “I love you” in response before the call cuts off.

When she looks up from her desk, she finds Scarlet on her phone too, quickly typing something before putting it away.

“Taja?”

Scarlet nods but doesn’t say anything more.

 

 

 

 

So it really shouldn’t have come as a surprise when Kara comes home and finds not one, but two people waiting for her.

Three, counting Scarlet.

“Lillian? Taja? she says, still standing at the doorway, staring at Lillian and Taja, who have made themselves comfortable in the living room.

Taja inexplicably wears a pastel blue dinosaur onesie with the hood up, framing her tiny face. Her brows are furrowed, eyes focused on her laptop. Beside her, looking just as comfortable, is Lillian, hair in a loose pony, wearing silk pajama pants and a comfortable white shirt. The Luthor matriarch is on her phone.

They both glance at her quickly before returning to what they were doing as if it shouldn’t surprise Kara that they were both there…or that they both looked like they were settling in for the night.

And, again, by now, it really shouldn’t have.

“Lena called me first,” Lillian says, not looking up from her phone, as if informing Kara of a discussion that she and Taja has been having before she arrived.

“Yes, but I was here before you,” Taja replies, also not looking up from her laptop.

Kara sighs, placing her bag down on a nearby counter, toeing her shoes off. She looks at Scarlet who is pointedly avoiding her stare. She narrows her eyes at her before heading to the kitchen.

“Oh, don’t bother with dinner, dear,” Lillian says, finally looking up from her phone.
“Nia, Brainy, and Winn and James are on their way with takeout.”

“Okay… who else did Lena call?”

Taja shrugs. “Everyone, I suppose.”

“Alex and Kelly are a little bit miffed they can’t be here. Alex has a couple of important meetings and Kelly has her appointments full this week,” Lillian supplies with a smile.

“And why did Lena say you needed to be here?”

“She didn’t,” Taja answers.

Lilian puts her phone down and starts walking towards Kara with that distinct knowing smile. “She called me because it’s my business to know these things. Taja she probably called for Scarlet and as far as I know, she called Nia because Nia was planning ‘Game Night’ and Lena had to cancel.”

Kara frowns. She does remember Nia mentioning something about someone else planning Game Night.

“I’m guessing Nia called everyone else to inform them about cancelling the entire thing,” Lilian continues, shrugging. “And they all just randomly decided to have dinner here instead… much to Alex’s and Kelly’s disappointment.”

“Game Nights are Fridays,” Kara says, still frowning.

“Are you actually mad that your friends came to visit you?” It’s a gentle sort of question without her usual barb.

“No… Just…” Kara searches for the word for a second. “Surprised, I guess?”

Lilian looks at her for a second. “Well, don’t be. You have shit taste in men. Fortunately, that does not extend to your choice in friends.” She motions Kara out of the kitchen. “Go shower and get changed so you can put your feet up and ‘chill with us’… as my assistant likes to phrase it.”

“I got you something, by the way,” Taja adds, still on her laptop. She leans over one side of the couch and Kara can see her blindly grasping at something until Scarlet strolls over and gets it for her.

“The same onesie?” Kara says when Scarlet hands it over.

“It’s a slightly more realistic green. I don’t want to be in a onesie by myself,” Taja says, typing something on her laptop before finally turning to Kara. “I lost a bet,” she adds.

Kara looks at Scarlet who is trying to hide a smug grin.

“And you want me to suffer with you?” Kara asks as she starts to walk out of the kitchen. She hears Lilian stifling a laugh.

“I don’t know if suffer is the term I’d use. This is surprisingly comfortable. It might be a bit big for you, but I suppose that’s the point,” Taja answers.

By now, Kara has reached the door to her and Lena’s bedroom.  “What are the terms of your… uhmmm… bet?”

Taja shrugs. “I’m afraid I cannot disclose such things. But asking you to wear one is not breaching its terms.”

Kara shakes her head.

 

 

Despite being initially hesitant to wearing the onesie, after a muscle-melting bath, she shrugs and decides to go for it, much to Nia’s joy and Taja’s approval. She must admit, it’s much softer and more comfortable than she expected. The tail is adorable.

“I like you. You’re my new best friend,” Nia tells Taja over dinner once she’s gotten over squealing at Kara when she came out of their bedroom. Taja smiles back. “Which means you must come to Game Night.”

The unwavering amused smirk Taja has grows a touch wider. “Thank you. I will come whenever I can.”

“I’m hearing a ‘No’,” Nia says, frowning.

“You’re hearing a ‘maybe’,” Taja explains.

“Just be glad you’re not hearing excuses,” Lillian pipes in.

Taja doesn’t answer but inclines her head at Lillian.

“So, Scarlet works for you? But she’s with Kara?” Brainy asks, changing topics.

“Yes,” Taja simply answers, which seems to satisfy Brainy’s curiosity.

Kara’s only so glad that her friends are focused on and fascinated with Taja so that the discussion is almost entirely centered on her. Taja answers everything as evenly (and slyly evasive) as she can, despite Lillian seemingly unable to stop from poking fun at her from time to time. 

So far, Kara hasn’t said much, watching from the sidelines and enjoying this rare moment of calm in her life. She looks at everyone gathered at her dining table. Friends. People she never thought she’d actually have. People who seem to like being around her for no particular reason.

“Sometimes, people leave us for reasons beyond our and their understanding. And it’s very human, entirely normal, to feel abandoned. It’s not anyone’s job to tell you how you should feel about certain things, certainly not mine. But it is also important to acknowledge those who stay,” she can almost hear her therapist say as she sits back, listens, and sips her apple juice.

 

 

Eventually, everyone goes home except Taja, who claims Kara’s old bedroom as hers. Needless to say, Scarlet is with her. (“The bed’s big enough, Kara Danvers,” Taja reasons when Kara tries to sleep on the couch to accommodate Scarlet.)

Lena video calls her once she’s ready for bed. She tells Lena about her day, about Nia’s new bestie, about Lillian in silk pajama pants, about Brainy and Taja seemingly able to understand each other with lengthy questions and one-word answers, about James and Winn bickering like an old married couple. She shows off her onesie and Lena promises to buy her more. That evening, Kara falls asleep watching Lena working a world away.

There’s a faint sound of a car rushing past and Kara wakes up, cold and uneasy, in the middle of the night. She blindly grasps towards Lena’s side of the bed only to remember a second later that Lena’s not coming home. Not for two nights.

Like those weekends a long time ago…

Her heart sinks for a second before it races painfully. She shakes her head, closing her eyes, breathing slowly. She focuses on counting all her exhales until she reaches ten before opening her eyes.

In the darkness, barely lit by the soft neon of the clock by her bedside, Kara sits up, willing her thoughts, her fears, her anxieties back down. She counts her slow exhales again, her eyes locked on her shaking palms.

After a few seconds, she decides to get up. Her heart still racing, her skin clammy, she hurriedly changes out of the onesie and into a plain shirt and the most comfortable jeans she has. She thinks of where she'd left her bag as she opens the bedroom door.

Sometimes, it really is just easier to—

“Can’t sleep?”

Kara nearly jumps, her heart painfully rattling around in her ribs, incessant thoughts interrupted by a figure lit entirely by a laptop screen, staring at her, amethyst eyes tired but curious.

“Taja?”

She shrugs, still in her onesie. “I had to make sure Scarlet was asleep before I resumed working. Otherwise, she won’t go to bed and then who’ll drive you later?” A pause. Before Kara can answer, Taja continues. “I suppose in that case I‘ll drive you tomorrow. That should be fun. At the very least, I’ll have a great excuse to dodge a few meetings in the morning.” She tilts her head, eyes narrowed.

Kara’s anxiety rises. She can almost hear Taja’s thoughts, as the smaller woman quietly observes her. She takes a step towards the door, seeing her bag on the counter nearby.

“Hmm. You know what? That’s actually a fantastic idea,” Taja says, standing up. She stretches, reaching her arms up as high as she can before walking towards Kara. “But maybe let’s not bring a lot of money.”

Kara frowns. “What?”

She watches as Taja, still in her comfortable onesie, quietly picks up a couple of things beside her. The other woman then walks toward Kara and offers her arm. “Come on,” she says, her voice even quieter now. “I don’t want to wake Scarlet up. Trust me.”

Taja pulls on her until they are out of the apartment and in front of Taja’s personal car. To Kara’s surprise, Taja tosses her the keys. She looks at Taja and then at the keys.

“Where are we going?” she asks.

“Wherever you were running off to,” Taja says before ducking in the passenger seat.

 

 

 

 

Notes for the Chapter:

Hi there!
Apologies for this being a year late.
For those who follow me on Twitter, you know I've been referencing my uncooperative brain from time to time. And really, that's all that's been happening. If it weren't for the patience, kindness and generosity of good friends, I wouldn't even find the energy to get up and get on with my day.

But I would also like to say that the comments and the kudos I receive give me that boost that I need so I thoroughly appreciate them, and you.

I won't make any promises but know that I'm not giving up on this story and that I have at least a couple more stories I'm slowly working on.
